PQC Today capability coverage matrix

PQC Today executes selected public reference vectors, standards tests, conformance cases, and implementation probes. A passing result is evidence only for the identified test, operation, parameters, implementation build, and target. It is not an ACVTS verdict, a CAVP/CMVP certificate, or proof of exhaustive conformance.

Generated from the runtime mechanism inventory, the reviewed capability map and the registered test inventory. A status says a registered test exists for the cell; it is not a pass. Machine-readable copy: coverage-matrix.json.

Headline

  • C++: 1635 advertised capability cells (165 mechanisms). Positive: 170 covered, 627 sampled, 838 untested. Negative: 63 covered, 39 sampled, 1533 untested. 28 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 923 cells with a recorded pass, 441 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).
  • Rust: 1025 advertised capability cells (172 mechanisms). Positive: 173 covered, 347 sampled, 505 untested. Negative: 63 covered, 39 sampled, 923 untested. 638 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 641 cells with a recorded pass, 556 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).

Rules and definitions

cell
A capability cell is one (mechanism, operation, parameter set, sign variant). Mechanism, operation (from CKF_ flags) and key-size range are DISCOVERED from the running engine (C_GetMechanismList + C_GetMechanismInfo); parameter sets, sign variants and boundaries are DECLARED in capability-map.json and kept only when the parameter set falls inside the engine-reported key-size range.
denominator
Denominator (per engine) = advertised capability cells: the engine lists the mechanism, sets the CKF_ flag for the operation, and the declared parameter set falls inside its reported key-size range. Cells the engine does not advertise, and declared capabilities no PKCS #11 mechanism can express, are counted separately as unsupported and shown — never dropped.
status
Cell status = the strongest evidence class among registered test cases that exercise the cell on that engine (order: acvts-issued > nist-reference > standard-kat > oracle > differential > round-trip > behavior-only). No registered case = untested. Not advertised = unsupported. A status says a test EXISTS; it is not a pass.
covered
Covered (per polarity) = at least 2 distinct registered cases of that polarity exercise the cell AND, for positive, at least one of them checks an externally expected value (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat or oracle). A cell is covered overall only when positive, negative, boundary and state-error are all covered.
sampled
Sampled = at least one registered case, but the covered rule is not met. One happy-path case is always "sampled", never "covered" (plan Q5).
polarity
Polarities are counted separately: a positive case never counts toward negative, boundary or state-error. Boundary = a registered case whose recorded parameters hit a declared boundary of the cell (e.g. context length 0 or 255, empty message); it may also be a positive or negative case. State-error = a case asserting a PKCS #11 state or argument error.
parity
Parity = both engines have a RECORDED PASS of the same registered case on the same artifact kind. Skipped, unsupported and not-run never count as a pass, and a recorded result counts only for the exact artifact (sha256) the mechanism inventory records. Rows with no such pair are "not established".
artifacts
Artifact kinds are reported separately: wasm (the two shipped WebAssembly engines — registered tests run in the browser), native, hardware. Native and hardware targets have not been run: they are "not run" and never counted as pass.
dimensions
Plan G-3 — two further columns per cell, from the same registered cases. Algorithm correctness = cases whose expected value is checked (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat, oracle, differential, round-trip; state-error cases excluded); covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one has an externally expected value. PKCS #11 API behaviour = cases that assert only PKCS #11 behaviour (behavior-only evidence: return codes, operation state, buffers, attributes) plus every state-error case; covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one is a state-error case. Neither column says a test passed.
inventory
Per engine and artifact kind the matrix counts, separately: advertised cells (the denominator — C_GetMechanismList × operation × parameter set × variant), registered cells (≥ 1 registered case, by evidence status), cells with a recorded pass, with a recorded fail, and with a recorded skip (the engine did not advertise a mechanism the case needs, so it was not run), unsupported cells (not advertised) and untested cells (advertised, no registered case). A skip is never a pass and is never folded into untested.
numerator
Numerators (per engine, per polarity) = advertised cells whose level is covered, sampled or untested under the rules below; covered + sampled + untested = the denominator. Per-status numerators count advertised cells by their strongest registered evidence class.

Artifact kinds and parity

  • wasm — registered; partly recorded. Registered tests target the shipped WebAssembly engines. Recorded results: 4502 from errorPathProbes (every registered error-path case, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 6fdcd961b); 94 from katRunner (every registered KatKind) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 6fdcd961b); 2044 from useAcvpSuite (all categories, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 6fdcd961b). Tests without a recorded result are never reported as passed.
  • native — not-run. macOS, Linux x86-64 and Linux Arm64 native builds have not been run through these tests (plan H-2). Never counted as pass.
  • hardware — not-run. i.MX 95 and KV260 targets have not been run through these tests (plan H-3). Never counted as pass.

Rust engine accepts every invalid NIST ML-KEM encapsulation/decapsulation key (18 of 18)openunassignedD1-5Rust (wasm)—The NIST ACVP ML-KEM encapDecap VAL key-check cases (mlkem_encapdecap_val_test: modified H, ek values too large) are accepted by the Rust engine: its C_EncapsulateKey / C_DecapsulateKey paths perform no FIPS 203 §7.2 encapsulation-key or §7.3 decapsulation-key input checks (bundle 417c47a2; unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main). The workbench rows stay failing on Rust (acvp.07b.keycheck). Since gap-closure P5 the 12 further invalid encapsulation keys of mlkem_ekcheck_depth_test (every remaining testPassed=false case of the upstream encapsulationKeyCheck groups) are accepted as well (acvp.07c.ekcheck-depth); the C++ engine rejects all 18. The fix is on pqctoday-hsm fix/mlkem-input-checks-0925 and reaches these rows with the next WASM rebuild. Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
C++ HashSLH-DSA wraps M′ twice — valid NIST pre-hash signatures rejectedopenunassignedD3-3C++ (wasm)—OSSLSLHDSA.cpp (bundle 7795799b, lines ~341-402) builds M′ = 0x01 ‖ len(ctx) ‖ ctx ‖ OID ‖ PH(M) itself and then passes it to OpenSSL's pure SLH-DSA via EVP_DigestSign together with OSSL_SIGNATURE_PARAM_CONTEXT_STRING, so OpenSSL wraps it again as a pure message: all 5 valid NIST pre-hash sigVer signatures are rejected and deterministic pre-hash signatures differ at byte 0. Unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main. Rows stay failing on C++ (acvp.09c). Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
ML-KEM wrong-length inputs: return codes differ from PKCS #11 v3.2 and between enginesopenunassignedD1-5 / G-8C++ + Rust (wasm)—Wrong-length ciphertext at C_DecapsulateKey: Rust returns CKR_ENCRYPTED_DATA_INVALID (not in the return-value list of PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.18.9), C++ returns CKR_WRAPPED_KEY_LEN_RANGE. The C++ engine accepts a decapsulation key 1 byte short at C_CreateObject; both engines accept a short encapsulation key. The boundary rows (acvp.07b.boundary) pin each engine's observed code, so a pass there means 'behaves as pinned', not 'conforms'.
PBKDF2 iteration floor differs between engines (Rust refuses c < 1000)openunassignedG-9C++ vs Rust (wasm)—The Rust engine refuses CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 with iterations < 1000 (rust/src/ffi.rs:11125 at 417c47a2: CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); the C++ engine accepts c = 1 (SoftHSM_keygen.cpp ~3012-3030 at 7795799b). A policy divergence, not an algorithm error. The c = 1 oracle cases (pbkdf2_test tests/0) are therefore not registered; katRunner and the workbench run the c = 4096 cases, which pass on both engines.
ECDSA P-521 NIST sample failed on C++ — hub CKA_EC_POINT encoding (fixed hub-side)in-progressunassignedE (ECDSA) / J-7C++ (wasm) — hub adapter—Root cause (triage 2026-09-24): hsm_importECPublicKey wrote the DER OCTET STRING length of the 133-byte P-521 point as the single byte 0x85, which DER reads as '5 length octets follow'; the C++ engine parsed a garbage CKA_EC_POINT and rejected the NIST ACVP-Server ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 sample (workbench §33), while Rust passed because it reads the raw CKA_VALUE copy. Hub adapter bug, not an engine defect. Fixed by the X.690 long-form length (softhsm/helpers derOctetString); the re-recorded run shows the case passing on both engines. Kept on record until the release freeze re-captures evidence on the browser host.
SUCI key-injection helper is rejected by the C++ engineopenunassignedA-3C++ (wasm) — hub helper—hsm_injectTestKey unwraps the raw EC scalar with C_UnwrapKeyAuthenticated using a template that carries CKA_EC_PARAMS; the C++ engine rejects it with CKR_ATTRIBUTE_READ_ONLY (the same unwrap without CKA_EC_PARAMS succeeds). katRunner suci-profile-b steps 1-2 (import only, not registered) therefore error on C++; steps 3-7 import with C_CreateObject and pass on both engines. The KAT surfaces run the Rust engine. Hub helper limitation.
C++: sign/verify inits accept a key of the wrong type (CKR_OK instead of CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT)openunassignedG-8C++ (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 7795799b)—C_SignInit and C_VerifyInit with an AES secret key return CKR_OK for 33 mechanisms (CKM_ECDSA and the 9 CKM_ECDSA_SHA*, CKM_EDDSA, CKM_RSA_PKCS, CKM_RSA_X_509, CKM_RSA_PKCS_PSS and every CKM_*_RSA_PKCS / CKM_*_RSA_PKCS_PSS hash variant); C_MessageSignInit / C_MessageVerifyInit do the same for the 30 ECDSA/RSA message-sign mechanisms; C_DeriveKey(CKM_HKDF_DERIVE) accepts an EC private key. PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.13.1, §5.15.1, §5.14.1, §5.16.1, §5.18.5 list CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and §5.1.6 defines it ("The specified key is not the correct type of key to use with the specified mechanism"). ML-DSA/SLH-DSA/HMAC/CMAC inits reject the same key correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.

Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.

Rust: Edwards / Montgomery key-pair generation accepts an inconsistent CKA_KEY_TYPEopenunassignedG-8Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2)—C_GenerateKeyPair(CKM_EC_EDWARDS_KEY_PAIR_GEN, Ed25519/Ed448) and (CKM_EC_MONTGOMERY_KEY_PAIR_GEN, X25519/X448) with CKA_KEY_TYPE = CKK_RSA in the public template return CKR_OK. §5.18.2: a template key type inconsistent with the mechanism "fails and returns the error code CKR_TEMPLATE_INCONSISTENT". Every other key-generation mechanism refuses correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
Rust: advertised cells whose operation does not executeopenunassignedG-8Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2)—The G-2/G-8 probes found advertised capability cells that fail on a valid key: CKM_ECDSA_SHA1 and CKM_ECDSA_SHA224 — C_SignInit CKR_OK then C_Sign CKR_MECHANISM_INVALID on P-256, P-384, P-521 and secp256k1; CKM_ECDSA (raw) on P-521 with a 32-byte input — C_Sign CKR_FUNCTION_FAILED (§6.3 allows any input length for raw ECDSA); AES-192 (inside the advertised 16–32 byte range) — CKM_AES_CBC_PAD C_Encrypt returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and CKM_AES_GCM C_MessageEncryptInit CKR_KEY_SIZE_RANGE; CKM_EC_KEY_PAIR_GEN_W_EXTRA_BITS on secp256k1 (in the advertised 256–521 range) — CKR_CURVE_NOT_SUPPORTED. Either the operation or the advertisement (C_GetMechanismInfo range / flags, plan G-7) is wrong. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
PKCS #11 v3.2 gives no single key-handle / key-type code for C_DecapsulateKey — not probedopenunassignedG-8C++ + Rust — specification—§5.18.9 lists CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID and CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT (not CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID / CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT), while §5.1.6 says the UNWRAPPING_* codes "can only be returned by C_UnwrapKey". With no single specified value there is no key-handle or key-type probe for C_DecapsulateKey (the engines differ: C++ returns CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID for handle 0, Rust CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID). Likewise C_WrapKey, C_UnwrapKey, C_DeriveKey and C_EncapsulateKey require the usage attribute but do not list CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED, so no key-usage probe exists for them. Specification ambiguity, not an engine defect. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
Rust engine refuses AES-GCM IVs other than 96 bitsopenunassignedE (AES-GCM) / G-9Rust (wasm)—The NIST ACVP-Server ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 sample (aesgcm_acvp_test, workbench §1b) has two groups with a 120-bit IV (tg2 encrypt, tg4 decrypt). The Rust engine refuses them at C_EncryptInit/C_DecryptInit with CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID (rust/src/ffi.rs ~8158 at 7795799b: 'AES-GCM requires a 12-byte nonce'); SP 800-38D and PKCS#11 v3.2 §6.13.2 allow other IV lengths and the C++ engine passes all 60 cases. The 30 Rust rows fail, including tg4's upstream authentication failures, which are refused before the tag is checked and therefore do not count as rejections. Engine limitation or advertisement to narrow — a decision for the engine owner.
Rust engine accepts P-224 keys but cannot verify P-224 ECDSA signaturesopenunassignedE (ECDSA) / G-7 / G-9Rust (wasm)—ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 P-224 groups (ecdsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4b): C_CreateObject accepts the P-224 public key, then every C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE for the 56-byte r||s — the Rust crate set has no P-224 (no p224 dependency at 7795799b) although CKM_ECDSA* advertise ulMinKeySize 112. All 28 Rust P-224 rows fail (valid cases rejected; invalid cases refused for the wrong reason, which the section does not count as a rejection). C++ passes all 112 ECDSA cases. P-224 is also outside the capability map's EC curve set, so these cases map to no capability cell; their results are recorded.
HMAC keys below the advertised ulMinKeySize are accepted by both enginesopenunassignedG-1 / G-7C++ + Rust (wasm) — advertisement—C_GetMechanismInfo advertises ulMinKeySize = the digest length in bytes for every CKM_<hash>_HMAC[_GENERAL] (e.g. 32 for SHA2-256), yet both engines compute and verify NIST HMAC 2.0 cases with shorter keys (down to 1 byte) correctly (hmac_acvp_matrix_test, workbench §2b; each row records keyInAdvertisedRange). FIPS 198-1 allows any key length, so the results are correct; the advertisement understates what the engines accept. Recorded, not enforced.
Classical NIST reference samples cover only what the pinned upstream samples registeropenunassignedEC++ + Rust—The pinned ACVP-Server@975de31e samples bound what WS-E can claim: ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 registers AES-128 only (IV 96/120, tag 128/32, payload/AAD 0/120 bits) — AES-192/256-GCM, 64-bit tags and other lengths have no NIST case; HMAC 2.0 registers macLen 80..160 bits — no full-length (untruncated) tag, so CKM_<hash>_HMAC (non-general) has no NIST case; EDDSA-SigVer-1.0 carries no context string (contexts are exercised only through the EDDSA-SigGen-1.0 byte-match cases of eddsa_siggen_acvp_test); ECDSA SHA2-512/256 and SHAKE groups have no PKCS#11 v3.2 CKM_ECDSA_<hash> mechanism (skip rows). Checked again 2026-09-25 (GitHub API, gap-closure P5): 975de31e (2026-08-12, "RELEASE/v1.1.0.43 including hotfix patches") is still the head of usnistgov/ACVP-Server master and the only other branch (jbrock24-patch-1, 2024) changes the README only, so no newer upstream commit carries AES-192/256-GCM or full-length HMAC tag samples; the scope gap stays.
Rust engine answers a wrong-length AES-CBC IV with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BADopenunassignedG-8 / G-9Rust (wasm)—Product-authored probe aescbc-probe-iv15 (workbench §12b): C_EncryptInit(CKM_AES_CBC) with a 15-byte IV. The C++ engine returns CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID; the Rust engine returns CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, which PKCS#11 v3.2 §5.8.1 does not list for C_EncryptInit (CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID is the listed code for a bad mechanism parameter). The Rust row stays red; the probe pins the spec code for both engines.
Rust engine cannot verify RSA signatures whose public exponent exceeds 2^33 - 1openunassignedE (RSA) / G-9Rust (wasm)—RSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 (rsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4d): the upstream 4096-bit PKCS#1 v1.5 group (e = 0xC9986A9C84FEE9, 56 bits) and the 2048-bit PSS/SHA3-256 group (e = 0x0ACC245201D531, 52 bits) import, but every C_Verify returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT on the Rust engine — rsa::RsaPublicKey::new (rust/src/crypto/handlers.rs ~2895 at 7795799b) refuses exponents above 2^33 - 1. FIPS 186-5 §A.1.1 allows 2^16 < e < 2^256. 12 Rust rows fail (valid cases rejected, invalid ones refused for the wrong reason); C++ passes all 24. The groups were chosen by the deterministic subset policy (first group per combination), not by result.
Rust engine's CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 implements only the HMAC-SHA-256/384/512 PRFsopenunassignedE (PBKDF2) / G-7 / G-9Rust (wasm)—PBKDF 1.0 (pbkdf2_acvp_test, workbench §18b): the pinned NIST sample registers HMAC-SHA2-224 only. The Rust engine answers CKP_PKCS5_PBKD2_HMAC_SHA224 with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD (rust/src/ffi.rs ~11138 at 7795799b: prf match arms SHA256/384/512 only, every other PRF -> CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 is advertised without any PRF restriction. All 5 Rust rows fail; C++ passes all 5, including the 1-iteration case that the Rust iteration floor (pbkdf2-min-iterations-divergence) would refuse anyway.
KMAC C_Verify accepts only the default MAC length, whatever ulOutputLen saysopenunassignedE (KMAC) / G-9C++ + Rust (wasm) — vendor mechanism—KMAC-128 1.0 MVT (kmac_acvp_test, workbench §35b): the valid case tc799 has a 478-byte MAC; both engines accept CK_PQCTODAY_KMAC_PARAMS.ulOutputLen = 478 at C_VerifyInit but C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE because the signature length is checked against the mechanism's fixed default (32 bytes for KMAC-128; rust/src/ffi.rs ~7295-7336 at 7795799b lists CKM_KMAC_128/256 among the fixed-length MACs). The invalid case tc771 (a 32-byte MAC) is rejected with CKR_SIGNATURE_INVALID on both. The pinned KMAC-256 sample has no byte-aligned non-XOF case, so KMAC-256 still has no NIST case (the older kmac_test KMAC256 case stays quarantined). Vendor mechanism, both engines.
Classical families still without NIST ACVP-Server cases after WS-E and gap-closure P5openunassignedEC++ + Rust—Closed by gap-closure P5 (2026-09-25): HKDF (hkdf_acvp_test, KDA-HKDF-Sp800-56Cr2), SP 800-108 KBKDF (kbkdf_acvp_test, KDF 1.0 counter / feedback / double pipeline), ECDSA/EdDSA KeyVer (ecdsa_/eddsa_keyver_acvp_test, the key is used: sign with d, verify with the point), ECDSA SigGen verify-back (functional round-trip + independent oracle; NIST r, s are not reproducible because C_Sign draws its own nonce), EdDSA SigGen byte-match, the alternate-version SHA-2 MCT and full 100-iteration MCTs for SHA-2/SHA-3 and AES-CBC (sha_mct_full_test, aescbc_mct_full_test). Still open: ANSI X9.63 KDF on a caller-supplied Z — not expressible through PKCS #11 (declaredUnreachable x963-kdf-caller-z, gap x963-kdf-caller-z-no-mechanism); SHA LDT (1 GiB expanded message); HKDF multi-expansion groups; KBKDF with CMAC-TDES, HMAC-SHA2-512/224, /256 or bit-oriented output lengths; ECDSA/EdDSA KeyGen and deterministic ECDSA SigGen (no PKCS #11 input for the random / RFC 6979 nonce). The older HKDF/PBKDF2/X9.63/KBKDF workbench rows keep their published-standard / oracle / round-trip labels.
ANSI X9.63 KDF on a caller-supplied shared secret has no PKCS #11 mechanismaccepted-limitationunassignedEC++ + Rust (specification limit)—PKCS #11 v3.2 defines the X9.63 KDF only as the CK_EC_KDF_TYPE of CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE and related ECDH mechanisms (§6.3.16), applied to the shared secret the token derives from its own key pair; no mechanism accepts a caller-supplied Z, so the NIST kdf-components-ansix9.63-1.0 cases (Z, sharedInfo -> keyData) cannot be executed. Shown as the workbench skip row x963-kdf-unsupported and the capability-map declaredUnreachable row x963-kdf-caller-z. The KDF itself is exercised inside ECDH derivation (section 25, round-trip only).
C++: SP 800-108 KDFs ignore where the caller places the counter in the PRF inputopenunassignedEC++ (wasm)—With the PKCS #11 v3.2 §6.42 data-parameter layout the NIST KDF 1.0 cases require, the C++ engine derives the wrong key whenever the counter is not in its fixed position: counter mode with the ITERATION_VARIABLE after or in the middle of the fixed data, and feedback / double-pipeline with a COUNTER before the chaining value or after the fixed data (kbkdf_acvp_test, 12 rows per run: HMAC-SHA2-256 and CMAC-AES128). 'Before fixed data' (and no counter) layouts byte-match NIST for every PRF. The engine appears to place the counter first (counter mode) or right after K(i-1)/A(i) (feedback / double pipeline) regardless of the CK_PRF_DATA_PARAM order. Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
Rust: feedback and double-pipeline KBKDF reject the mandatory ITERATION_VARIABLE data parameteropenunassignedERust (wasm)—PKCS #11 v3.2 §6.42.4/§6.42.5 make CK_SP800_108_ITERATION_VARIABLE mandatory in feedback and double-pipeline mode (pValue NULL, ulValueLen 0; §6.42.2). The Rust engine returns CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID for every such layout, so all 38 feedback / double-pipeline NIST KDF 1.0 rows fail. Probed 2026-09-25: without the ITERATION_VARIABLE entry the engine accepts the parameters and reproduces NIST except where the counter precedes the chaining value. Counter mode (ITERATION_VARIABLE with a counter format) is correct in every location. The hub keeps the specification layout; the existing sections 27/29 call the feedback mechanism without the ITERATION_VARIABLE. Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
C++: invalid ECDSA / EdDSA public points are imported and used without validationopenunassignedEC++ (wasm)—The NIST keyVer cases with a point not on the curve or a coordinate out of range (ecdsa_keyver_acvp_test P-256/384/521, eddsa_keyver_acvp_test Ed25519/Ed448: 10 points) are accepted by C_CreateObject and C_VerifyInit, and C_Verify then returns CKR_SIGNATURE_INVALID as for any wrong signature — no FIPS 186-5 / SP 800-56A public-key validation happens on import or use (PKCS #11 v3.2 §4.1.1 would return CKR_ATTRIBUTE_VALUE_INVALID at creation). Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
Rust: invalid public points are refused only at C_Verify, with a code C_Verify does not listopenunassignedE / G-8Rust (wasm)—The Rust engine imports the 10 invalid NIST keyVer points (C_CreateObject CKR_OK) and accepts C_VerifyInit, then C_Verify returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT — not among the §5.15.2 C_Verify return values.
